NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, April 10, 2018
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, April 10, 2018, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters as indicated on the following agenda. The Board may meet in executive session pursuant to
A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3) for legal advice on any item.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office
at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, with the exception of items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo
County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to 5
minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any comments
but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be placed on a
future agenda.
 
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature and
will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be removed
from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: March 27, 2018  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000, March 2018  
 
c. Constable Reports: Winslow Precinct #2, February; Snowflake Precinct #3, March; Show Low
Precinct #5, March; and Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, March 2018
 
 
d. Personnel Actions  
 
e. Forest Fees Allocations for the 2017-2018 year  
 
f. 2018 Fair Housing Proclamation, proclaiming April as "Fair Housing" month in Navajo County  
 
g. Application for Treasurer's Deed for the Board of Supervisors on behalf of the State of Arizona  
 
h. Approval of 1 Back Tax Land Parcel sold over the counter in the amount of $25,415  
 
i. Approval of 12 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $8,300.56  
 
j. Approval of the use of the southwest corner of the Navajo County Complex for the annual

<!-- page 2 -->
j. Approval of the use of the southwest corner of the Navajo County Complex for the annual
community July 4th Fireworks Display
 
 
k. Amended Facilities Agreement Between Navajo County, and Arizona Conservation Corps for a
portion of the Pine Lake Facility located at 1852 S.& Pine Lake, Pinetop, Arizona
 
 
l. Tax exemptions filed pursuant to ARS §42-11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver  
 
m. Name an existing unnamed road in the Overgaard Area as "Trailblazer Drive" in Section 21,
Township 12 North, Range 17 East
 
 
n. Name an existing unnamed road in the Pinetop Lakeside Area as "Old Dirt Road" in Section 9,
Township 9 North, Range 17 East
 
 
o. Rename an existing road in the Adamana Area from "Twenty First Street" to "Woodcrafter
Way" in Section 1, Township 18 North, Range 23 East
 
 
p. Appointment of Terry Lee Graham, Virginia Lee Gardner, Patricia A. Graham, Dennis James
Azevedo, and Stetson Eugene Kizzar as Republican Precinct Committeemen for their respective
precincts
 
 
q. Amendment Number One to the Ellen Morse Professional Services contract to perform
psychiatric services for the Adult Detention facility
 
 
r. Modification 002 to U.S. Forest Service agreement 17-LE-11030121-010 adding $10,000 for the
2018 Annual Operating Plan
 
 
s. Application for Extension of Premises/Patio Permit for Kimberly Ann Mathews of Mr. Earl's
Pizza Time for permanent change of area of service to increase the available seating for
restaurant customers and provide an area for live entertainment
 
 
3. SHERIFF'S OFFICE:  
 
a. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring April 8 - April 14, 2018 as "National
Telecommunicator's Week". 
 
Presented By: Sheriff KC Clark/Chief Deputy Moffitt
 
4. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring the month of April 2018 "Child Abuse Prevention &
Awareness Month".
 
Presented By: Chairman Steve Williams/C.J. Wischmann
 
b. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring the week of April 14 - 20, 2018 as the Week of the
Young Child.
 
Presented By: Supervisor Dawnafe Whitesinger/Jessica Beach

<!-- page 3 -->
5. COUNTY ATTORNEY:  
 
a. Presentation of the 7th Annual Sue's Crew 30 Mile Walk for Cancer to be held on May 12,
2018.  
 
Presented By: Jim Hillebrecht and Anna Atencio
 
6. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Consideration of Livingston Family Funeral Services LLC, bid for Mortuary, Body Storage and
Transportation Services.
 
Presented By: Jeff Lee
 
7. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of Approving Task Agreement No. 4 to Memorandum of Agreement between
Hopi Department of Transportation and Navajo County for the Funding of Navajo County
Maintenance activities which will not exceed the amount of $30,000 from District ll Hopi CIP
Funds. 
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
b. Consideration of the BIA Western Agency Task Agreement No. 9 for the purchasing of
aggregate material for BIA Route N2 not to exceed $80,000.00. 
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
c. Consideration of the Hopi Tribe Department of Transportation Task Agreement No. 5 for the
purchase and delivery of aggregate material for Sand Springs Road NR/IR 9751, expenditure
not to exceed $20,000.00.
 
Presented By: John Osgood
 
8. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and Board
members.
 
 
 
9. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:   
 
a. EXECUTIVE SESSION pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (1) for personnel discussion to
conduct the annual performance evaluation of the Clerk of the Board 
 
 
b. RECONVENE for action related to the annual evaluation of the Clerk of the Board.

<!-- page 4 -->
10. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: April 5, 2018 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted:________ By: MB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Title: Approval for the Distribution of the 2018 Forest Fee Allocation
Submitted For: Jalyn Gerlich, Superintendent of Schools

Forest Fees Allocations for the 2017-2018 year

The Board of Supervisors has approved that the Forest Fees, Secure Rural Schools fund be

district allocations available for their approval.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.f.        
Meeting Date: 04/10/2018  
Title: 2018 Fair Housing Proclamation
Submitted For: Paige Peterson, Finance Director 
Submitted By: Jackie Walker, Senior Account Specialist
Department: Administrative Services Division: Finance
Motion before the Board:
2018 Fair Housing Proclamation, proclaiming April as "Fair Housing" month in Navajo County
Background:
The Civil Rights Act of 1968 and Fair Housing Amendments Act of 1988 declare a national
policy prohibiting discrimination in the sale, rental, leasing, and financing of housing or land to be
used in the construction of housing or in the provision of brokerage services on the basis of race,
color, religion, sex, disability, familial status, or national origin. The Housing and Community
Development Act of 1974 as amended requires that all applicants for Community Development
Block Grant funds certify that they shall affirmatively further fair housing. Navajo County’s
proclamation and resolution declaring April 2017 Fair Housing Month for Navajo County and the
dissemination thereof will reaffirm the County’s commitment to furthering fair housing. 
Attachments
Fair Housing Proclamation 
Form Review
Inbox Reviewed By Date
Paige Peterson Paige Peterson 03/29/2018 01:05 PM
Paige Peterson Paige Peterson 03/29/2018 01:16 PM
Paige Peterson Paige Peterson 03/29/2018 01:27 PM
Form Started By: Jackie Walker Started On: 03/29/2018 12:32 PM
Final Approval Date: 03/29/2018

<!-- page 408 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
FAIR HOUSING PROCLAMATION 
 
 
 
HEREAS, The National Fair Housing Law of 1986, as amended by the Fair 
Housing Amendments Act of 1988 prohibits discrimination in housing and 
declares it a national policy to provide within constitutional limits, for fair 
housing in the United States; and 
 
 
HEREAS, the principle of Fair Housing is not only national law and national 
policy but a fundamental human concept and entitlement for all Americans; 
and 
 
 
HEREAS, April has traditionally been designated as Fair Housing Month in 
the United States; 
 
 
 
OW, THEREFORE, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, do hereby 
proclaim April as Fair Housing Month in Navajo County and do hereby urge all  
Citizens of this community to comply with the letter and spirit of the Fair 
Housing Law. 
 
 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on April 10, 2018. 
 
 
 
     _____________________________________________ 
     Steve Williams, Chairman 
 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
_____________________________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.g.        
Meeting Date: 04/10/2018  
Title: Application for Treasurer's Deed for the State of Arizona
Submitted For: Debra Kester, Treasurer  Submitted By: Alonna Larson, Account
Clerk
Department: Treasurer
Motion before the Board:
Application for Treasurer's Deed for the Board of Supervisors on behalf of the State of Arizona
Background:
APPROVAL BY THE BOARD PER ARS 42-18261, wherein it reads: If a lien is not redeemed at
any time beginning five years after the lien is assigned to this state, the county board of
supervisors, acting on behalf of this state, may apply for and receive a treasurer's deed to the
property under this article.
Title searches have to be performed on each parcel, and each owner has to be served with a
certified notice.  If redemption be not made by law before August 31st, 2018, the parcels will then
be deeded to the state of Arizona, and turned over to the Board of Supervisors for auction.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2018-19
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
Approximately $38,200.00, in the Treasurer's Budget

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.k.        
Meeting Date: 04/10/2018  
Title: Amended Navajo County Lease Agreement for AZ Corps-AZCC
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Amended Facilities Agreement Between Navajo County, and Arizona Conservation Corps for a
portion of the Pine Lake Facility located at 1852 S.& Pine Lake, Pinetop, Arizona
Background:
Amendment: AZCC shall have access and occupy the Premises according to the subject property
map of the Premises, more particularly described on Exhibit "A" attached hereto and made a part
hereof.
This is a lease for the Pine Lake Facility with the Conservation Legacy dba Arizona Conservation
Corps AZCC. This lease allows the AZCC to have a staging and deployment area for their
operations in the region. On that same property, Public Works will continue to utilize a separate
building and there is a fuel site there, as well.
Attachments
Amended Lease for AZCC

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.l.        
Meeting Date: 04/10/2018  
Title: Approval of Individual Tax Exemptions with waiver 
Submitted For: Cammy Darris, Assessor 
Submitted By: Marlene Sample, Chief Deputy Assessor
Department: Assessor
Motion before the Board:
Tax exemptions filed pursuant to ARS §42-11153 as a Request for Redemption of Waiver
Background:
Background: (why should it be done, what will happen if not approved, etc. include resolution).  Pursuant to ARS
§42-11153, “If a widow, widower or disabled person whose property is exempt from tax under section §42-1111, the
person may have the waiver redeemed by the County Board of Supervisors at any regular meeting, except that no
taxes that were due and payable before the petition was submitted may be refunded or abated.” The following
applications for tax exemption were filed past the March 1 deadline, therefore, pursuant to ARS 42-11153, a “Request
for Redemption of Waiver” has been signed by each of the following:
        
Cantin, Barbara  409-25-056A Jodie, Yvonne N  103-06-023A
Ramirez, Alfred  309-30-014 Seitz, Russell  210-18-009

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.m.        
Meeting Date: 04/10/2018  
Title: Name an unnamed existing road in the Overgaard area to "Trailblazer Drive"
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Julianna Davis, GIS/E911 Addressing/Row Analyst
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Name an existing unnamed road in the Overgaard Area as "Trailblazer Drive" in Section 21,
Township 12 North, Range 17 East
Background:
The road is in Section 21, Township 12 North - Range 17 East. (See attached maps) Map 6-08 and
survey 23-72. Currently, this road serves 6 properties. In order to effectively address for E911 an
access should be named when 3 or more properties use the same access. All property owners
whose property is adjacent to the road are in support of the new road name and have signed off on
the Petition. The road is not County maintained.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.o.        
Meeting Date: 04/10/2018  
Title: Rename an exisiting road in the Adamana area from "Twenty First Street" to
"Woodcrafter Way"
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Julianna Davis, GIS/E911 Addressing/Row Analyst
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Rename an existing road in the Adamana Area from "Twenty First Street" to "Woodcrafter
Way" in Section 1, Township 18 North, Range 23 East
Background:
The road is in Section 1, Township 18 North- Range 23 East. (See attached maps) Map 1-13, Plat
9-36 & Amended Plat 9-45. Property owner Lloyd Johnson (106-02-200 & 201) requested a
Petition to change the name of the road from "Twenty First Street" to be called "Woodcrafter
Way". Mr. Johnson contacted 11 of the 12 owners that are adjacent to the road and received 8 of
the 11 owners response in support of the name change. Three of the 11 property owners did not
respond. Navajo County then sent a certified letter to the final property owner who was not
approached (106-02-252) and asked if they Approved or Opposed of the road name. We received
a response and they opposed  the name change. 
In summary, there are 8 property owners in support, 3 not responding, and 1 opposed to changing
Twenty First Street to Woodcrafter Way. The road is not County maintained.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2018
Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
No Fiscal Impact is expected

Board of Supervisors Regular 2.p.        
Meeting Date: 04/10/2018  
Title: Appointment of Republican Committeemen
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Appointment of Terry Lee Graham, Virginia Lee Gardner, Patricia A. Graham, Dennis
James Azevedo, and Stetson Eugene Kizzar as Republican Precinct Committeemen for
their respective precincts
Background:
Appointment valid through Oct. 1, 2018.
16-822. Precinct committeemen; eligibility; vacancy; duties; term
B.  If the number of persons who file nominating petitions for an election to fill precinct
committeeman positions is less than or equal to the number of precinct committeeman positions,
the county board of supervisors may cancel the election for those positions not sooner than
seventy-five days before the election and appoint the person who filed the nominating petition to
fill the position. If no person has filed a nominating petition to fill a position, the position is
deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A precinct committeeman who is
appointed pursuant to this subsection after filing a nominating petition shall be deemed an elected
precinct committeeman.
F. The term of office of a precinct committeeman is two years and begins on October 1 after the
primary election at which the precinct committeeman was a candidate and continues until October
1 after the following primary election at which a precinct committeeman is elected.

Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a Proclamation declaring April 8 - April 14, 2018 as "National
Telecommunicator's Week". 
Background:
Navajo County Board of Supervisors publicly honors the men and women whose diligence and
professionalism keep our county and citizens safe. 
Recommendation: 
Recommend Approval

Telecommunicator's Proclamation 
Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 04/02/2018 11:15 AM
Final Approval Date: 04/02/2018

<!-- page 499 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
National Telecommunicator's Week  
April 08th through April 14th  
Whereas emergencies can occur at any time that require police, fire or emergency medical services; and 
Whereas when an emergency occurs the prompt response of police officers, firefighters and paramedics is 
critical to the protection of life and preservation of property; and 
Whereas the safety of our police officers and firefighters is dependent upon the quality and accuracy of 
information obtained from citizens who telephone the Navajo County Sheriff’s police-fire communications 
center; and 
Whereas Public Safety Dispatchers are the first and most critical contact our citizens have with emergency 
services; and 
Whereas Public Safety Dispatchers are the single vital link for our police officers and firefighters by monitoring 
their activities by radio, providing them information and insuring their safety; and 
Whereas Public Safety Dispatchers of the Navajo County Sheriff’s Office have contributed substantially to the 
apprehension of criminals, suppression of fires and treatment of patients; and 
Whereas each dispatcher has exhibited compassion, understanding and professionalism during the 
performance of their job in the past year.  
Now, Therefore Be It Proclaimed, by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County and the Navajo County Sheriff 
that the week of April 8th through April 14th, 2018 is designated to be National Telecommunicator's Week in 
Navajo County, Arizona in honor of the men and women whose diligence and professionalism keep our city 
and citizens safe. 
Passed, adopted and approved by the Board of Supervisors on April 10th, 2018  
_________________________________ 
Steve Williams, Chairman of the Board

<!-- page 501 -->
Navajo County               
Proclamation 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS PROCLAMATION 
April 2018 “Child Abuse Prevention & Awareness Month” 
 
WHEREAS, Buena Vista Children’s Services, Inc. is a community -based, grassroots, nonprofit 
organization that offers child abuse prevention awareness, children’s advocacy and child care 
recruitment services for the Department of Economic Security, Education & Professional 
Development, in Navajo, Apache and Yavapai counties; and 
 
WHEREAS, every child is entitled to be loved, cared for, secure and safe, and it is the 
responsibility of our society to protect every child’s inalienable right to life, liberty and the pursuit 
of happiness; and 
 
WHEREAS, the hidden epidemic of child abuse, which crosses all boundaries of income, race, 
religion and ethnicity, continues to be one of our most serious public health issues, with over 90 
percent of abuse perpetrated by people that children know, love and trust; and 
 
WHEREAS, children who survive abuse endure lasting physical, mental, and emotional scars, 
including feelings of shame, self -doubt, poor self -image, the inability to trust others, learning 
disabilities, sleep disturbances, dating violence, difficulty in trust and coping skills, and eating, 
speech or post-traumatic stress disorders; and 
 
WHEREAS, child abuse and neglect can be reduced by providing safe havens and emotional, 
social, legal and financial support for victims and at -risk families; and by holding offenders 
accountable, we can ensure that all of Arizona’s children grow to their full potential as healthy 
citizens; and 
 
WHEREAS, providing the proper support and enhancing a child’s understanding of his or her own 
self-worth are crucial components in providing a bright future and raising healthy Arizona children. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, by the Board of Supervisors, on behalf of Navajo County, do her eby 
proclaim the month of April 2018, as “Child Abuse Prevention & Awareness Month” in Navajo 
County and urge all citizens to cherish our children and protect them from harm. 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on April 10, 2018. 
 
 
____________________________ 
Steve Williams, Chairman 
ATTEST: 
 
_______________________________ 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk of the Board

First Things First White Mountain Apache, Navajo/Apache, and Navajo Nation Regions and other

Navajo County               
Proclamation 
 
 
  Week of the Young Child 
 
WHEREAS, the First Things First White Mountain Apache, Navajo/Apache, and Navajo Nation 
Regions and other local organizations, in conjunction with the National Association for Education 
of Young Children, are celebrating the Week of the Young Child™, April 14 - 20, 2018; and 
WHEREAS, these organizations are working to improve early learning opportunities, including 
early literacy programs, that can provide a foundation of learning for children in Navajo County; 
and 
WHEREAS, teachers and others who make a difference in the lives of young children in Navajo 
County deserve thanks and recognition; and 
WHEREAS, public policies that support early learning for all young children are crucial to young 
children’s futures; 
NOW, THEREFORE, we, the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County, do hereby proclaim the week 
of April 14-20, 2018, as the Week of the Young Child in Navajo County, Arizona, and encourage 
all citizens to work to make a good investment in early childhood in Navajo County. 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on April 10, 2018.

CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES 
Contract #F18-02-08 
 
Agreement made as of April 10, 2018 between Navajo County (“County”) and Livingston 
Family Funeral Services LLC dba Owens Livingston Mortuary (“Consultant”). 
 
Whereas, the County requires certain professional services to provide Mortuary, Body 
Storage and Body Transportation Services.  
 
Whereas, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
Now, Therefore, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Navajo County Health District is responsible for medical examiner services 
which include Transportation, Cold Storage, and use of a facility to perform 
examinations. Please refer to bid F18-02-08 for a breakdown of services and equipment 
available for use.  
 
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, capacity 
and professional expertise to perform this agreement.  Consultant shall self-perform this 
agreement and be devoted to the successful accomplishment of the work to be 
performed hereunder.  Once work commences on this agreement, consultant shall 
adhere to agreed upon timeline and commitment to successful completion of the 
contracted services. 
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall be an agent, employee, partner, joint venturer or 
associate of the other.  An employee or agent of one party shall not be deemed an 
employer or agent of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social Security 
contributions will not be withheld from any payment by the County, and Consultant shall 
be solely responsible for such matters. 
 
4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of the 
work to be performed hereunder without the County’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed on 
Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 9, 
10, 12, 15 and 18.  Consultant is responsible for full performance of this Agreement 
regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same profession 
currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services shall be

<!-- page 508 -->
performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care and the 
orderly progress of the Project, and in accordance with the agreed upon schedule for the 
performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a schedule approved 
by the County shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by County or 
Consultant.  The schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project proceeds.  The 
date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days that performance is 
delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other causes beyond the 
Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the parties’ 
representatives. 
 
6. Payment.  Livingston Family Funeral Services LLC, will send the Navajo County Health 
District a monthly bill for services. Please refer to bid F18-02-08 for a breakdown of 
services and equipment available for use and the accepted cost for each.     
 
7. Term of Contract.  This contract will be for a three-year term, beginning April 10 th, 2018 
and ending on April 1, 2021 
 
8. Defects in Work.  County may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifications.  Consultant agrees to promptly remedy 
all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal negotiation as set forth in Section 12.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any rejected work until such issues have been resolved. 
 
9. Insurance Requirements.  Insurance requirements for this contract are: 
Workers’ Compensation by statute 
Professional Liability in the amount of $1,000,000 per incident / $1,000,000 
aggregate 
Commercial General liability of $1,000,000 bodily injury and property damage 
 
   
10. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable by law, Consultant shall defend, indemnify 
and hold harmless County and its representatives, officers, officials and employees (the 
“Indemnitees”) from and against all claims, damages, losses and expenses (including 
but not limited to attorneys fees, court costs, and the costs of appellate proceedings) 
relating to, arising out of or resulting from Consultant’s negligent acts, errors, mistakes or 
omissions in the performance of this Agreement.  Consultant’s duty to defend, hold 
harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise in connection with any claim, 
damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily injury, sickness, disease, death or 
injury to, impairment or destruction of property including loss of use resulting therefrom 
cause whole or in part by any negligent act, error, mistake or omission in the 
performance of this Agreement (including those by any person for whose negligent acts, 
errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s 
negligence or fault.  The amount and type of insurance coverage requirements set forth 
above shall not limit the scope of the indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
11. Records.  Consultant shall retain and shall require each subcontractor (if applicable) to 
retain, all books, accounts, reports, files and other records relating to this Agreement for 
a period of five years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be subject 
to inspection and audit by County upon reasonable notice during normal business hours.

<!-- page 509 -->
A legible copy of any or all such documents shall be produced by Consultant at the 
request and expense of County. 
 
12. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right in this Agreement without 
County’s prior written approval, nor shall Consultant delegate any duty hereunder 
without County’s prior written approval. 
 
13. Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any claim 
or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or controversy 
shall be provided in writing, with supporting documentation, to the recipient designated in 
Section 21.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to prepare and deliver a 
written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes within a reasonable period of 
not less than ten days, either party may pursue available legal remedies. 
 
14. Suspension and Termination by County: 
 
a. Suspension.  County may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made to 
the completion date and for any increase in cost of performance resulting from the 
suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the extent that 
performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted by another 
cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
b. Termination for Cause.  County may terminate this Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to perform this Agreement; disregards laws, ordinances, 
rules, regulations or orders of a public authority having jurisdiction; or otherwise 
commits a material breach of this Agreement.  When any of the foregoing causes 
exist, County shall give Consultant written notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure 
the default.  If the default remains uncured, County may (without prejudice to any 
rights or remedies it may have) terminate this Agreement and finish the work or 
cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of the Agreement exceeds the cost of 
finishing the work (including expenses incurred by the County as a result of the 
Consultant’s default), Consultant shall be entitled to payment for its performance up 
to the amount of such excess.  If the cost of finishing the work exceeds the unpaid 
balance, Consultant shall pay the difference to County. 
 
c. Termination for Convenience.  County, by written notice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of County, it is 
in County’s best interests to do so.  Consultant shall be paid for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and a 
reasonable allowance from profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shall not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any payments previously made to Consultant.  Consultant 
shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and services 
which were not provided. 
 
d. Consultant’s Responsibility.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant shall 
(a) promptly discontinue all services (unless notice directs otherwise), and (b) deliver 
or otherwise make available to County copies of all data, reports, summaries and

<!-- page 510 -->
other information and materials developed or accumulated by Consultant in 
performing this Agreement. 
 
15. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to County if performance is made impossible for a period of thirty (30) consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or its 
agents, employees or subcontractors:  issuance of an order by a count or other public 
authority having jurisdiction; an act of government, such as a declaration of national 
emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also terminate this 
Agreement upon written notice and a ten-day opportunity to cure if County fails to make 
any payment within time set forth in Section 6.  Consultant may also terminate this 
Agreement upon written notice if suspensions, delays or interruptions by County equal in 
the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of days scheduled for completion. 
 
16. Governing Law.  This Agreement shall be governed by the law of the State of Arizona.  
Any suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of Arizona (with 
venue in Navajo County) or federal District of Arizona, but only after informal negotiation 
pursuant to Section 12. 
 
17. Incorporation of Applicable Laws.  Every provision of law required by statute or 
regulation to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included herein.  
Each party shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such provision has 
been omitted. 
 
18. Non-Collusion.  The provisions A.R.S. 38-511 are incorporated herein by this reference. 
 
19. One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Consultant on any 
claim based upon or arising out of this Agreement unless such action is commenced 
within one year after the County’s final payment hereunder. 
 
20. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement and those documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understanding between the parties.  No amendment shall be 
effective unless executed by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.  
Provided, however, that the County’s designated representative is authorized to modify 
the Scope of Work, in writing, with the concurrence of Consultant so long as the project 
is not changed substantially, or significant additional compensation is not required. 
 
21. Severability.  The provisions of this Agreement are severable.  Any provision held to be 
invalid or unenforceable shall not affect the validity or enforceability of any other 
provision. 
 
22. HB 2151 – Iran Investments A.R.S § 35-397 
 
a. Prohibits the State and its political subdivisions from purchasing (goods or services) 
from a company with scrutinized business o perations in Iran and requires a 
Consultant to certify that the Consultant does not have scrutinized Business 
Operations in Iran.

23. HB 2151 – Sudan Investments and Business Operations A.R.S § 35-397 
 
a. Prohibits the State and its political subdivisions from  purchasing (goods or services) 
from a company with scrutinized business operations in Sudan and requires a 
Consultant to certify that the Consultant does not have scrutinized Business 
Operations in Sudan. 
 
 
24. Notices.  All notices, invoices and payments shall be in writing and may be given by 
personal delivery or certified mail.  The designated recipients are as follows:

Board of Supervisors Regular 7.a.        
Meeting Date: 04/10/2018  
Title: Hopi 60 Residential Millings
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Miles Begay, Engineering Tribal Trans Manager
Department: Public Works
Presented By: John Osgood
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Approving Task Agreement No. 4 to Memorandum of Agreement between Hopi
Department of Transportation and Navajo County for the Funding of Navajo County Maintenance
activities which will not exceed the amount of $30,000 from District ll Hopi CIP Funds. 
Background:
The Memorandum of Agreement between the Hopi Department of Transportation, Hopi Tribe and
Navajo County facilitates road maintenance activities between the County and the Hopi Tribe. 
The County Capital Improvement Plan includes funding for road maintenance project within the
tribe's system of inventoried roads.
The Hopi 60 route is a well traveled road that sees an estimate of 100+ vehicles a day; it connects
the East Hopi Communities with cental Navajo Nation. The Hopi Transportation Director has
been in contact with the County to make this project a priority.  The first half mile that connects
with SR 264 is a residential route that has in the past been improved to a milling surface due to
dust issues for residents.  The segment is in need of further maintenance.  Due to existing road
conditions travelers now drive on the side of the road in the bar ditch causing residents to bring
issues to the Hopi Transportation Director about health concerns with dust control and vehicle
safety for residents entering the Hopi 60.  In keeping with the existing MOU, the Hopi DOT has
requested resources from Navajo County to improve the existing milling surface and utilize a
stabilizing binding material that will extend the life of the road surface. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends that the Board of Supervisors approve the attached Task Agreement between
Hopi DOT and Navajo County. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2018
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $30,000
Fiscal Impact:

<!-- page 540 -->
The not to exceed amount of $30,000 will be funded from District ll Hopi CIP Funds.
